through Patterson Park, but now I just sit in my chair and none of it feels worth getting dressed for.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Food tastes like cardboard to me, and my skirts have gotten loose because most days I only pick at toast or tea even when my niece puts a plate right in front of me.\n- key: S027\n  name: Sleep_Disturbance\n  description: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.\n  manifestation: I lie awake half the night going over what I did wrong, then if I do sleep I’m up before dawn with my heart pounding and can’t settle back down.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest even in church the words in the hymnal blur together so I lose my place.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before my friend died and I sank into this, I never believed the choir or pastor were sending warnings about me. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, doctor… I don’t want to sound foolish or like I’m making trouble. I just… I’ve been feeling like everything is my fault. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

My friend died, and I keep telling people it’s because I didn’t call her back. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, too, and I think… I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know that sounds terrible. Is it alright to say that here?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having these ugly thoughts repeatedly, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I’m afraid I do. I try not to say it too much, because I know it sounds… wrong, maybe dramatic. But it keeps coming back that I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back. And then I remember I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I think the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know that must sound awful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I have to do something exactly. I mostly just try to push them away or tell myself not to think like that. Sometimes I pray, but… I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: When you have these ugly thoughts, do you feel like you need to think of something else to counteract them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I do that in any organized way. I might try to think about something nicer, or tell myself to stop, but I don’t know if it really counteracts it. I’m sorry, I’m not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts come into your mind without you wanting to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t sit there trying to think them, I promise. They just come in, even when I’m trying to be normal or do something else. It’ll be that thought again, that I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back… and then it turns into me thinking the Lord is punishing people at church because of me. I know that sounds terrible. I wish I could just not have it come into my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific actions to try to make these thoughts go away or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not specific actions, no. I mean, I might pray or try to distract myself, but I don’t have a ritual or anything I must do to stop something bad from happening. At least, I don’t think I do. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I have. I’ve tried to tell myself it’s just grief and that I should be handling it better, but it’s been most of the time, really. It’s been about two months now, since my close friend died. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must think certain thoughts or say certain words to yourself to make the ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have to, no… not like certain exact words or thoughts. Sometimes I’ll try to tell myself, “stop it” or “that’s not true,” but it doesn’t feel like a rule I have to follow to keep something from happening. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to repeat certain actions or mental acts in response to these ugly thoughts?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I get an overwhelming urge like that. I mean, I might try to push the thoughts away or keep myself busy, but I’m not repeating things over and over, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that… does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to neutralize or balance out the ugly thoughts with other thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a deliberate way. I might try to think of something nicer or tell myself it’s not right to think that way, but I don’t feel like I’m balancing it out exactly. I’m sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

My friend died, and I keep telling people it’s because I didn’t call her back. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, too, and I think… I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know that sounds terrible. Is it alright to say that here?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having these ugly thoughts repeatedly, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I’m afraid I do. I try not to say it too much, because I know it sounds… wrong, maybe dramatic. But it keeps coming back that I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back. I know that must sound awful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I have to do something exactly. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must think certain thoughts or say certain words to yourself to make the ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have to, no… not like certain exact words or thoughts. Sometimes I’ll try to tell myself, “stop it” or “that’s not true,” but it doesn’t feel like a rule I have to follow to keep something from happening. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to repeat certain actions or mental acts in response to these ugly thoughts?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I get an overwhelming urge like that. I mean, I might try to push the thoughts away or keep myself busy, but I’m not repeating things over and over, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that… does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to neutralize or balance out the ugly thoughts with other thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a deliberate way. I might try to think of something nicer or tell myself it’s not right to think that way, but I don’t feel like I’m balancing it out exactly. I’m sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are deliberately trying to counter or make up for the ugly thoughts with other thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing that on purpose, no. I might try to distract myself or think, “please don’t think that,” but it’s not like I have a set thing I do to make up for it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to get rid of the ugly thoughts by doing or thinking something specific?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything specific I do, no. I mostly just… try not to focus on them, maybe keep busy or let them pass if I can. But it’s not like I have a certain thought or action I use to get rid of them. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are coming into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t sit there and invite them in, if that’s what you mean. They just sort of show up sometimes, and I don’t like it. But I don’t know if I’d say “against my will” exactly… I’m sorry, I’m trying to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to make the ugly thoughts go away by thinking or doing something to counteract them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I mean, I might try to push them aside or tell myself not to dwell on it, but I don’t have, like… a special thing I do to cancel them out or anything. If that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I know that must sound awful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I have to do something exactly. I mostly just try to push them away or tell myself not to think like that. Sometimes I pray, but… I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: When you have these ugly thoughts, do you feel like you need to think of something else to counteract them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I do that in any organized way. I might try to think about something nicer, or tell myself to stop, but I don’t know if it really counteracts it. I’m sorry, I’m not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts come into your mind without you wanting to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t sit there trying to think them, I promise. They just come in, even when I’m trying to be normal or do something else. It’ll be that thought again, that I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back… and then it turns into me thinking the Lord is punishing people at church because of me. I know that sounds terrible. I wish I could just not have it come into my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific actions to try to make these thoughts go away or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not specific actions, no. I mean, I might pray or try to distract myself, but I don’t have a ritual or anything I must do to stop something bad from happening. At least, I don’t think I do. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I have. I’ve tried to tell myself it’s just grief and that I should be handling it better, but it’s been most of the time, really. It’s been about two months now, since my close friend died. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must think certain thoughts or say certain words to yourself to make the ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have to, no… not like certain exact words or thoughts. Sometimes I’ll try to tell myself, “stop it” or “that’s not true,” but it doesn’t feel like a rule I have to follow to keep something from happening. I’m not sure if I’m explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to repeat certain actions or mental acts in response to these ugly thoughts?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I get an overwhelming urge like that. I mean, I might try to push the thoughts away or keep myself busy, but I’m not repeating things over and over, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that… does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to neutralize or balance out the ugly thoughts with other thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a deliberate way. I might try to think of something nicer or tell myself it’s not right to think that way, but I don’t feel like I’m balancing it out exactly. I’m sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are deliberately trying to counter or make up for the ugly thoughts with other thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing that on purpose, no. I might try to distract myself or think, “please don’t think that,” but it’s not like I have a set thing I do to make up for it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to get rid of the ugly thoughts by doing or thinking something specific?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything specific I do, no. I mostly just… try not to focus on them, maybe keep busy or let them pass if I can. But it’s not like I have a certain thought or action I use to get rid of them. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are coming into your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t sit there and invite them in, if that’s what you mean. They just sort of show up sometimes, and I don’t like it. But I don’t know if I’d say “against my will” exactly… I’m sorry, I’m trying to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to make the ugly thoughts go away by thinking or doing something to counteract them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I mean, I might try to push them aside or tell myself not to dwell on it, but I don’t have, like… a special thing I do to cancel them out or anything. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are trying to undo or cancel out the ugly thoughts with other thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any kind of deliberate way. I might try to distract myself or think about something nicer, but I don’t have a certain thing I do to undo it or cancel it out. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

Is it alright to say that here?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having these ugly thoughts repeatedly, even when you try to stop thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… I’m afraid I do. I try not to say it too much, because I know it sounds… wrong, maybe dramatic. But it keeps coming back that I’m the reason my friend died, because I didn’t call her back. And then I remember I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I think the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know that must sound awful.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I have to do something exactly. I mostly just try to push them away or tell myself not to think like that. Sometimes I pray, but… I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: When you have these ugly thoughts, do you feel like you need to think of something else to counteract them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I do that in any organized way. I might try to think about something nicer, or tell myself to stop, but I don’t know if it really counteracts it. I’m sorry, I’m not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: Do these ugly thoughts come into your mind without you wanting to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yes… they do. I don’t sit there trying to think them, I promise. They just come in, even when I’m trying to be normal or do something else. It’ll be that thought again, that I’m the reason my friend died because I didn’t call her back… and then it turns into me thinking the Lord is punishing people at church because of me. I know that sounds terrible. I wish I could just not have it come into my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific actions to try to make these thoughts go away or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not specific actions, no. I mean, I might pray or try to distract myself, but I don’t have a ritual or anything I must do to stop something bad from happening. At least, I don’t think I do. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since your friend passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I have. I’ve tried to tell myself it’s just grief and that I should be handling it better, but it’s been most of the time, really. It’s been about two months now, since my close friend died. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must think certain thoughts or say certain words to yourself to make the ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I have to, no… not like certain exact words or thoughts. I mean, I might try to push them aside or tell myself not to dwell on it, but I don’t have, like… a special thing I do to cancel them out or anything.
